1. Tele MER waived till 65 years, will be conducted for age b/w 66 years and 70 years for fresh & portability business, applicable to both preferred and non-preferred geography

30/08/2025, 6:06 PM - +91 92469 34603: Insurance Ombudsman reports: These health insurers top the charts with maximum complaints against them - The Economic Times https://share.google/rngkfg0AJYRCs8u4R
30/08/2025, 6:06 PM - +91 92469 34603: Must read:
Here’s the gist of the final outcome from the Ombudsman report:

Star Health is by far the worst performer – the maximum complaints, highest rejection-related grievances, and the largest compensation ordered.

CARE Health and Niva Bupa also rank poorly, with relatively high complaints per lakh insured.

Among public-sector insurers, National Insurance has moderate complaints, while New India Assurance performs the best with the lowest complaint ratio.

Overall, complaints against health insurers are rising sharply (up ~22% in a year), unlike life and general insurance.

The regulator (IRDAI) is now pushing insurers to set up an internal ombudsman system, but concerns exist about impartiality since they’ll be on insurer payrolls.


👉 Bottom line:

Star Health shows systemic red flags in claims handling.

CARE Health and Niva Bupa need stronger controls.

Public-sector insurers, especially New India Assurance, appear more reliable in terms of complaint ratios.
30/08/2025, 6:06 PM - +91 92469 34603: Here is the gist of my research on the matter:

📊 Ombudsman Report – FY 2023-24 (Health Insurance)

Star Health
• Complaints received: 10,196
• Awards ordered: 7,506
• Compensation: ₹60.5 Cr

CARE Health
• Complaints received: 2,393
• Awards ordered: 1,687
• Compensation: ₹20.1 Cr

Niva Bupa
• Complaints received: 1,770
• Awards ordered: 1,297
• Compensation: ₹16.5 Cr

National Insurance
• Complaints received: 1,419
• Awards ordered: 1,015
• Compensation: ₹5.4 Cr

📌 Industry totals:
❌ Claims Rejected = ₹26,000 Cr
⚖️ Claims taken to Ombudsman = ~₹1,100 Cr

👉 Key Takeaway:
Most customers are not approaching the Ombudsman after claim rejection.
Only ~4% of rejected claims are even challenged.

Key points from the statement:
- Mr. Jain has been a policyholder since November 18, 2021.
- This is his third claim instance in the current year.
- ₹22.72 lakhs has already been paid in the last two instances.
- Details of the three claim instances:
    - April 23, 2025: 26-day stay, discharged May 19, ₹21,01,453 approved.
    - June 21, 2025: 2-day stay, discharged June 23, ₹1,71,478 approved.
    - June 27, 2025: 67+ days ongoing, discharge awaited, ₹25,00,000 approved.
- Niva Bupa approved ₹25 lakhs for the current treatment (admitted June 27) for 27 days.
- An additional ₹77,000 charge on July 9 wasn't approved.
- The hospital requested more pre-authorization (up to ₹61-80 lakhs), but this wasn't approved.
- Niva Bupa denies the claim was denied; ₹25 lakhs pre-authorization is valid.
- Final claim amount decided at discharge.
- Niva Bupa believes fear-mongering narratives labeling the health insurance sector a "scam" are irresponsible.
- Mr. Jain was a beneficiary twice this year.
- Niva Bupa intends to honor genuine claims.

05/09/2025, 7:04 PM - +91 98210 65975: HDFC Life has announced that the new GST exemption on individual insurance policies will come into effect on September 22, 2025. For any login or payment made from today until September 21, 2025, HDFC Life will refund the GST paid, but only if the policy conversion (issuance) occurs after September 22, 2025.

 *GST Refund Details* 
- GST paid between now and 21st September 2025 on HDFC Life logins or payments is eligible for a refund.

- Refund is applicable only if the case (policy) is converted—i.e., the policy is issued—on or after September 22, 2025, when the new zero GST regime takes effect.

 *Key Conditions* 
- Any payment or login activity before September 21, 2025, can get GST refunded, but only if the policy is actually issued after September 22.

- If the policy is issued before September 22, the GST refund will not apply, because the old GST rates are still in force until that date.

08/09/2025, 5:13 PM - Rohit Ji Mfd Noida: My experience on cataract

Incase of Catarat many plans like the ones above do not have a cap or sublimit defined. Insurer and Hospital contracts usually are signed for Monofocal Lens...which will cost between 30-40K. Hospitals usually push Multifocal lens which can be between 55-65K. Infact hospitals many times dont event give option for Monofocal, they simply will tell policy holder we will use Multifocal. 
Insurer at their end evaluates weather a person needs Multifocal or not. It could depend on profession or existing history.
08/09/2025, 5:17 PM - Sankalp Sengar Ji Beshak Advisor: There is no amt limit for cataract... Rather city wise and hospital wise limits for type of procedure and lens.

From my limited learning, only monofocal is covered. Multifocal difference has to be borne by consumers.
08/09/2025, 5:20 PM - +91 98116 11555: Yes. As a Standard Practice & Guidelines followed by the Companies only Monofocal Lenses are Paid by the Insurance Cos.

Multifocal Lenses / Femto laser Lenses Costs are not paid by the Companies.

On Monofocal Lenses even higher amounts are Paid by the Companies

12/09/2025, 7:22 PM - +91 99250 29859: *Out of Question?*
12/09/2025, 7:50 PM - +91 94229 27582: 1.	Nature of Insurance Premiums
	•	When you pay insurance premium (say for 3 years in advance), GST is charged at the time of payment, because insurance is treated as a supply of service under GST.
	•	GST liability arises at the time of supply (Section 13 of CGST Act). For insurance, the time of supply is usually when premium is received or due, whichever is earlier.
	2.	If GST Rate Later Becomes Nil
	•	The tax you already paid on past transactions (when the rate was applicable) cannot be refunded just because the government reduced the rate to 0% later.

	3.	Future Premium Periods
	•	Even though coverage is for 3 years, the supply is considered made at the time you paid (advance supply of service).
	•	So GST paid at that time was correct and valid.
	•	Therefore, you cannot claim refund for the next 2 years just because GST is now nil.

✅ Conclusion:
No GST refund will be available for the future coverage period. The rate cut will apply only to new premiums you pay now onward, not for past advance payments.

30/09/2025, 3:41 PM - +91 87660 67101: IMG-20250930-WA0165.jpg (file attached)
Friends, sooner or later our insurance industry may also move to a sub-broker / trail-based revenue model, just like what happened in the mutual fund industry. Back in 2009, when entry load was waived off, many feared it would kill the business. But in reality, it only separated the serious players from the non-serious ones. Those who stayed committed got a bigger share of the pie, because the MF industry became more transparent and investor-friendly.

If something similar happens in insurance, a trail-kind revenue model will actually be a game changer. It will bring more stability, ease of doing business, and long-term value creation for both clients and advisors.

So rather than fearing commission cuts, we should see this as an opportunity. Just like in mutual funds, the advisors who are serious, ethical, and service-oriented will only grow stronger. Time will tell, but I’m confident the future is bright for those who adapt.

Once upon a time:

Policy: ₹10,000 (above 60 years) → Agent earned ₹1,500

Now:

Commission slashed to 10% → ₹1,000

Deduct 18% further → Agent gets ₹847

Less TDS 2% → final take-home even lower 😢

Agents are the backbone of the insurance industry, yet the system thinks: “By killing the distributors, can we sustain for long?”
We sell protection… but who’s protecting the protector? 🕵️‍♂️💸

12/11/2025, 4:02 PM - +91 99305 02030: If this is the case, anyone aware how loading in applied in top up policies? Because the basic premiums are discounted premiums considering the deductible <This message was edited>
12/11/2025, 4:03 PM - +91 99305 02030: In top up policies also the loadings shouldn’t be applied on the basic premiums, they should be applied on the non discounted premium
12/11/2025, 4:06 PM - +91 87915 89757: *Basic premium is initially discounted due to deductible.*

*Loading is then applied on this discounted base premium to account for the risk profile.*

*This means loading does not negate the deductible discount but is added as a risk surcharge on the already discounted premium reflecting the insurer’s increased risk exposure due to the policyholder's profile or claim history.* <This message was edited>
12/11/2025, 5:28 PM - +91 92469 34603: Top-up policy is different. There is no base premium (because deductible is mandatory) and deductible premium separately.

Anyway, the decision is with the insurer on how to they apply loading

submit latest reports. If doctor has not advised knee replacement then it can be accepted with loading.

17/12/2025, 4:24 PM - +91 98707 72163: INSURANCE ACT AMENDMENT BILL | SABKA BIMA SABKI SURAKSHA BILL PASSED IN LOKSABHA 
⸻
*100% FDI IN INSURANCE*
* FDI limit raised from 74% to 100%
* Aims to attract foreign capital & boost insurance penetration
* Greater management control for foreign investors
* Indian presence mandatory in top leadership
* At least one Chairperson / MD / CEO must be a resident Indian
⸻
*CLASS OF INSURANCE BUSINESS DEFINED*
* Clear legal classification of insurance businesses
* Five classes recognized: Life, General, Health, Reinsurance & future classes
* Govt empowered to notify new classes in consultation with IRDAI
* Opens future scope for composite license
⸻
*IRDAI EMPOWERED ON COMMISSIONS*
* IRDAI to regulate commissions, remuneration & rewards
* Applies to agents & intermediaries
* Objective: curb mis-selling & protect policyholders
⸻
*IRDAI TO FRAME INVESTMENT RULES*
* Greater flexibility for insurers, especially life insurers
Life Insurance
* 50% in Government securities
* 25% Central Govt bonds
* 25% State Govt bonds
General Insurance
* 25% in Government securities
* 10% in Central & State Govt bonds
⸻
*INSURANCE & NON-INSURANCE MERGERS ALLOWED*
* Mergers permitted with IRDAI approval
* Enables corporate restructuring
* Positive for diversified financial groups
⸻
*REINSURER CAPITAL NORMS RELAXED*
* Capital requirement cut to ₹1,000 crore (from ₹5,000 crore)
* Applies to foreign reinsurers
* Boosts competition & capacity in reinsurance
⸻
*OPEN ARCHITECTURE NOT INCLUDED*
* Open architecture dropped from the Bill
* Individual agents restricted to single insurer
* Positive for large incumbents like LIC & PSU insurers
⸻
*POLICYHOLDERS’ EDUCATION & PROTECTION FUND*
* PEPF to be set up & administered by IRDAI
* Focus on awareness & policyholder protection
* Funded through Govt grants & regulatory penalties
⸻
*TRANSPARENT RULE-MAKING PROCESS*
* Mandatory public consultation
* Standardized regulatory framework
* Improves accountability
⸻

* Authority to recover wrongful gains
